========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I guess the main thing is this memorial I’ve been trying to put together for my brother. It started off as something simple, you know, just a way to honor him, maybe have some music and a few pictures, but it’s gotten much bigger than I meant for it to. I keep finding more things that need doing—songs he liked, people I should call, displays I could make, places tied to him I ought to visit or include somehow—and before I know it, whole days are gone.

My wife says I’ve been spending most of my time chasing all of that down, calling folks, planning little trips, arranging things in my head and on paper, and she says I’ve pushed aside everything else. The garden’s been sitting there, I haven’t been going with my walking group like I usually do, and honestly a lot of the regular day-to-day stuff has just kind of fallen away. So… that’s what brings me in, I suppose. It’s gotten hard to slow it down.

I’m not sure I’d call them “unwanted thoughts,” exactly. It’s more like once I start thinking about the memorial, one thing leads to another and then I’m off in it again. Like I’ll see something in the house, or remember a song, or think of someone who knew my brother, and then I start wondering whether that should be part of it somehow.

I can’t say I’ve really sat there trying not to think about it and had it force its way in, if that’s what you mean. It’s more… it just keeps taking up space because there’s always another detail, another idea. Sometimes I don’t even realize I’ve gone back to it until I’ve spent an hour making notes or looking something up. So, I don’t know. It keeps coming up, yes, but I’m not sure it feels like an intrusive thing in the way you’re asking.

I keep finding more things that need doing—songs he liked, people I should call, displays I could make, places tied to him I ought to visit or include somehow—and before I know it, whole days are gone.\n\nMy wife says I’ve been spending most of my time chasing all of that down, calling folks, planning little trips, arranging things in my head and on paper, and she says I’ve pushed aside everything else. Yeah… cheerful maybe isn’t exactly the word I’d use, but there are times lately where I feel, I don’t know, lit up. Like everything about this memorial suddenly feels so clear and important, like I’ve got this purpose and if I can just get it right, then people in town are finally going to understand my brother, and understand our family, and what all of it meant.

So… yes, there’s been this high feeling, but it’s mixed with me getting sharp with people too.

those things started along with this burst of energy and nonstop planning.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I’ve missed my walking group and book club, let the garden go, and upset friends at the senior center because everything has become about the memorial display. My wife says our days revolve around calming me down or redirecting me from another call, trip, or argument.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about two months, ever since my brother passed and I began working on his tribute.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 69 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S040\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess the main thing is this memorial I’ve been trying to put together for my brother. It started off as something simple, you know, just a way to honor him, maybe have some music and a few pictures, but it’s gotten much bigger than I meant for it to. I keep finding more things that need doing—songs he liked, people I should call, displays I could make, places tied to him I ought to visit or include somehow—and before I know it, whole days are gone.\n\nMy wife says I’ve been spending most of my time chasing all of that down, calling folks, planning little trips, arranging things in my head and on paper, and she says I’ve pushed aside everything else. The garden’s been sitting there, I haven’t been going with my walking group like I usually do, and honestly a lot of the regular day-to-day stuff has just kind of fallen away. So… that’s what brings me in, I suppose. It’s gotten hard to slow it down.\n\nDoctor: Do unwanted thoughts about the memorial keep popping into your mind even when you try not to think about it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call them “unwanted thoughts,” exactly. It’s more like once I start thinking about the memorial, one thing leads to another and then I’m off in it again. Like I’ll see something in the house, or remember a song, or think of someone who knew my brother, and then I start wondering whether that should be part of it somehow.\n\nI can’t say I’ve really sat there trying not to think about it and had it force its way in, if that’s what you mean. It’s more… it just keeps taking up space because there’s always another detail, another idea. Sometimes I don’t even realize I’ve gone back to it until I’ve spent an hour making notes or looking something up. So, I don’t know. It keeps coming up, yes, but I’m not sure it feels like an intrusive thing in the way you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually cheerful or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… cheerful maybe isn’t exactly the word I’d use, but there are times lately where I feel, I don’t know, lit up. Like everything about this memorial suddenly feels so clear and important, like I’ve got this purpose and if I can just get it right, then people in town are finally going to understand my brother, and understand our family, and what all of it meant.\n\nAnd when I’m in that feeling, it can feel almost good—more than good, really, like I’m carrying something that matters. But it doesn’t stay soft or pleasant. If my wife asks me to slow down, or says maybe the display doesn’t need another section, I can snap at her. Same with people at the senior center, if they question how I’m setting things up or they don’t move fast enough. I get irritated quick, like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\nSo… yes, there’s been this high feeling, but it’s mixed with me getting sharp with people too.\n\n[Last Question]\nLately, have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still feeling full of energy?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, my sleep has been a bit all over the place, but I haven’t been keeping track of it the way maybe I should. Some nights I’m up later than I meant to be because I get caught up thinking through details or writing something down, and then I look at the clock and it’s later than I expected.

But whether I’m needing much less sleep and still feeling full of energy… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t want to say that exactly if I don’t know. There are times I keep going when my wife thinks I should stop, and I’ll tell her I’m fine, I’ve got things to finish, but I also don’t know how much of that is energy and how much is just me not wanting to leave things undone.

So… maybe I’ve been staying up more, but I haven’t really noticed it as “I don’t need sleep anymore” or anything like that. It’s hard for me to separate.

It keeps coming up, yes, but I’m not sure it feels like an intrusive thing in the way you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually cheerful or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… cheerful maybe isn’t exactly the word I’d use, but there are times lately where I feel, I don’t know, lit up. Like everything about this memorial suddenly feels so clear and important, like I’ve got this purpose and if I can just get it right, then people in town are finally going to understand my brother, and understand our family, and what all of it meant.\n\nAnd when I’m in that feeling, it can feel almost good—more than good, really, like I’m carrying something that matters. But it doesn’t stay soft or pleasant. If my wife asks me to slow down, or says maybe the display doesn’t need another section, I can snap at her. Same with people at the senior center, if they question how I’m setting things up or they don’t move fast enough. I get irritated quick, like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\nSo… yes, there’s been this high feeling, but it’s mixed with me getting sharp with people too.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still feeling full of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, my sleep has been a bit all over the place, but I haven’t been keeping track of it the way maybe I should. Some nights I’m up later than I meant to be because I get caught up thinking through details or writing something down, and then I look at the clock and it’s later than I expected.\n\nBut whether I’m needing much less sleep and still feeling full of energy… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t want to say that exactly if I don’t know. There are times I keep going when my wife thinks I should stop, and I’ll tell her I’m fine, I’ve got things to finish, but I also don’t know how much of that is energy and how much is just me not wanting to leave things undone.\n\nSo… maybe I’ve been staying up more, but I haven’t really noticed it as “I don’t need sleep anymore” or anything like that. It’s hard for me to separate.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been feeling more important or specially capable than usual lately?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`.   },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure I’d put it that way. “More important” sounds a little… big, like I’m walking around thinking I’m better than people, and I don’t think that’s what I feel. At least I don’t notice myself thinking that.

I do feel like what I’m doing matters, maybe more than other people seem to understand, and sometimes that makes me feel like I’m the one who has to make sure it comes out right. But specially capable? I don’t know. I’ve always been particular about things when they matter to me. My wife might say I’m acting like I’m the only one who can handle it, but from inside it just feels like I’m trying to do it properly.

So… I guess I haven’t really noticed feeling powerful or superior or anything like that. More like I feel responsible, and maybe I’m holding on to it too tightly.

Risky? I don’t know… I guess I’m not sure what counts as risky in the way you mean.

I haven’t, like, gone out and done something wild on purpose, if that’s what you’re asking. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well… yes, I suppose that’s closer to what my wife has been worried about. I know how it’s going to sound when I say it out loud, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic, but there are these old ballads my brother kept—songs he cared about—and I’ve been going over them because of the memorial. And the more I listen, or read the words, the more I’m sure there are warnings in them.

Not just memories, not just “oh, this reminds me of him.” I mean warnings meant for me. Certain lines stand out in a way they didn’t before, like he left something there, or like I’m supposed to understand what to do from them. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, that they’re just songs and grief makes people connect things. I understand why she’d say that. But I know what I heard in those words. It isn’t random to me.

And I know you might say that sounds unrealistic, but it feels very clear when I’m with them. Like there’s a message tucked underneath the plain meaning, and if I ignore it, I’d be missing something important from my brother.

Nothing like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you started to believe things about the memorial or your brother that other people say aren’t true or seem unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose that’s closer to what my wife has been worried about. I know how it’s going to sound when I say it out loud, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic, but there are these old ballads my brother kept—songs he cared about—and I’ve been going over them because of the memorial. Certain lines stand out in a way they didn’t before, like he left something there, or like I’m supposed to understand what to do from them. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, that they’re just songs and grief makes people connect things. I understand why she’d say that. But I know what I heard in those words. It isn’t random to me.\n\nAnd I know you might say that sounds unrealistic, but it feels very clear when I’m with them. Like there’s a message tucked underneath the plain meaning, and if I ignore it, I’d be missing something important from my brother.\n\n[Last Question]\nDid all of this start after your brother died?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Yes… yes, it really did. I mean, not the very minute he died, if you know what I mean, but it all began after that. It’s been about two months now, since he passed, and especially since I started working on the tribute for him.

At first it just felt like normal grieving, trying to pull things together and make the memorial mean something. I was going through things connected to him, thinking about what would honor him properly, and then it sort of… grew from there. The songs, the meaning of them, the feeling that I needed to get it right—that all started in that period after his death.

So yes, I’d say it’s been going on for around two months, tied pretty directly to losing him and planning the memorial.

those things started along with this burst of energy and nonstop planning.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I’ve missed my walking group and book club, let the garden go, and upset friends at the senior center because everything has become about the memorial display. My wife says our days revolve around calming me down or redirecting me from another call, trip, or argument.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about two months, ever since my brother passed and I began working on his tribute.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 69 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S040\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you done anything lately that felt out of character or risky for you?\n\nPatient: Risky? I don’t know… I guess I’m not sure what counts as risky in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t, like, gone out and done something wild on purpose, if that’s what you’re asking. I’m not thinking of anything where I’d say, “That was completely unlike me and dangerous.” Mostly I’ve just been wrapped up in getting things right, and maybe I’ve pushed harder than people around me would like. But when I’m doing it, it doesn’t feel risky exactly—it feels necessary, or like I’m trying to finish what I started.\n\nOut of character… maybe my wife would have a better answer, honestly. She might say I’ve been more intense or stubborn than usual. But from my side, I can’t point to some big thing, like spending a bunch of money or taking off somewhere or doing something reckless. I don’t think so. Nothing like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you started to believe things about the memorial or your brother that other people say aren’t true or seem unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose that’s closer to what my wife has been worried about. I know how it’s going to sound when I say it out loud, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic, but there are these old ballads my brother kept—songs he cared about—and I’ve been going over them because of the memorial. And the more I listen, or read the words, the more I’m sure there are warnings in them.\n\nNot just memories, not just “oh, this reminds me of him.” I mean warnings meant for me. Certain lines stand out in a way they didn’t before, like he left something there, or like I’m supposed to understand what to do from them. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, that they’re just songs and grief makes people connect things. I understand why she’d say that. But I know what I heard in those words. It isn’t random to me.\n\nAnd I know you might say that sounds unrealistic, but it feels very clear when I’m with them. Like there’s a message tucked underneath the plain meaning, and if I ignore it, I’d be missing something important from my brother.\n\nDoctor: Did all of this start after your brother died?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it really did. I mean, not the very minute he died, if you know what I mean, but it all began after that. It’s been about two months now, since he passed, and especially since I started working on the tribute for him.\n\nAt first it just felt like normal grieving, trying to pull things together and make the memorial mean something. I was going through things connected to him, thinking about what would honor him properly, and then it sort of… grew from there. The songs, the meaning of them, the feeling that I needed to get it right—that all started in that period after his death.\n\nSo yes, I’d say it’s been going on for around two months, tied pretty directly to losing him and planning the memorial.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been talking more than usual lately?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think so. I don’t always notice it while it’s happening, but people have pointed it out.

Like at the senior center meetings, if we start talking about the memorial, I’ll begin explaining the songs, and then that leads into the quilts, and then the photos, and how each one fits with my brother, and before I know it I’ve gone on and on. I’m not trying to take over, but I’ll realize I’ve talked right over two or three people who were trying to add something.

My pastor has had to sort of gently step in and say, “Aaron, let’s let someone else speak,” which is embarrassing when I think about it afterward. At the time, though, it feels like I need to get the whole thought out before it disappears or before someone misunderstands what I mean. So yes, I suppose I have been talking more than usual.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess the main thing is this memorial I’ve been trying to put together for my brother. It started off as something simple, you know, just a way to honor him, maybe have some music and a few pictures, but it’s gotten much bigger than I meant for it to. I keep finding more things that need doing—songs he liked, people I should call, displays I could make, places tied to him I ought to visit or include somehow—and before I know it, whole days are gone.\n\nMy wife says I’ve been spending most of my time chasing all of that down, calling folks, planning little trips, arranging things in my head and on paper, and she says I’ve pushed aside everything else. The garden’s been sitting there, I haven’t been going with my walking group like I usually do, and honestly a lot of the regular day-to-day stuff has just kind of fallen away. So… that’s what brings me in, I suppose. It’s gotten hard to slow it down.\n\nDoctor: Do unwanted thoughts about the memorial keep popping into your mind even when you try not to think about it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call them “unwanted thoughts,” exactly. It’s more like once I start thinking about the memorial, one thing leads to another and then I’m off in it again. Like I’ll see something in the house, or remember a song, or think of someone who knew my brother, and then I start wondering whether that should be part of it somehow.\n\nI can’t say I’ve really sat there trying not to think about it and had it force its way in, if that’s what you mean. It’s more… it just keeps taking up space because there’s always another detail, another idea. Sometimes I don’t even realize I’ve gone back to it until I’ve spent an hour making notes or looking something up. So, I don’t know. It keeps coming up, yes, but I’m not sure it feels like an intrusive thing in the way you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually cheerful or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… cheerful maybe isn’t exactly the word I’d use, but there are times lately where I feel, I don’t know, lit up. Like everything about this memorial suddenly feels so clear and important, like I’ve got this purpose and if I can just get it right, then people in town are finally going to understand my brother, and understand our family, and what all of it meant.\n\nAnd when I’m in that feeling, it can feel almost good—more than good, really, like I’m carrying something that matters. But it doesn’t stay soft or pleasant. If my wife asks me to slow down, or says maybe the display doesn’t need another section, I can snap at her. Same with people at the senior center, if they question how I’m setting things up or they don’t move fast enough. I get irritated quick, like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\nSo… yes, there’s been this high feeling, but it’s mixed with me getting sharp with people too.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still feeling full of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, my sleep has been a bit all over the place, but I haven’t been keeping track of it the way maybe I should. Some nights I’m up later than I meant to be because I get caught up thinking through details or writing something down, and then I look at the clock and it’s later than I expected.\n\nBut whether I’m needing much less sleep and still feeling full of energy… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t want to say that exactly if I don’t know. There are times I keep going when my wife thinks I should stop, and I’ll tell her I’m fine, I’ve got things to finish, but I also don’t know how much of that is energy and how much is just me not wanting to leave things undone.\n\nSo… maybe I’ve been staying up more, but I haven’t really noticed it as “I don’t need sleep anymore” or anything like that. It’s hard for me to separate.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more important or specially capable than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. “More important” sounds a little… big, like I’m walking around thinking I’m better than people, and I don’t think that’s what I feel. At least I don’t notice myself thinking that.\n\nI do feel like what I’m doing matters, maybe more than other people seem to understand, and sometimes that makes me feel like I’m the one who has to make sure it comes out right. But specially capable? I don’t know. I’ve always been particular about things when they matter to me. My wife might say I’m acting like I’m the only one who can handle it, but from inside it just feels like I’m trying to do it properly.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed feeling powerful or superior or anything like that. More like I feel responsible, and maybe I’m holding on to it too tightly.\n\nDoctor: Have you done anything lately that felt out of character or risky for you?\n\nPatient: Risky? I don’t know… I guess I’m not sure what counts as risky in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t, like, gone out and done something wild on purpose, if that’s what you’re asking. I’m not thinking of anything where I’d say, “That was completely unlike me and dangerous.” Mostly I’ve just been wrapped up in getting things right, and maybe I’ve pushed harder than people around me would like. But when I’m doing it, it doesn’t feel risky exactly—it feels necessary, or like I’m trying to finish what I started.\n\nOut of character… maybe my wife would have a better answer, honestly. She might say I’ve been more intense or stubborn than usual. But from my side, I can’t point to some big thing, like spending a bunch of money or taking off somewhere or doing something reckless. I don’t think so. Nothing like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you started to believe things about the memorial or your brother that other people say aren’t true or seem unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose that’s closer to what my wife has been worried about. I’m not thinking of anything where I’d say, “That was completely unlike me and dangerous.” Mostly I’ve just been wrapped up in getting things right, and maybe I’ve pushed harder than people around me would like. But when I’m doing it, it doesn’t feel risky exactly—it feels necessary, or like I’m trying to finish what I started.\n\nOut of character… maybe my wife would have a better answer, honestly. She might say I’ve been more intense or stubborn than usual. But from my side, I can’t point to some big thing, like spending a bunch of money or taking off somewhere or doing something reckless. I don’t think so. Nothing like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you started to believe things about the memorial or your brother that other people say aren’t true or seem unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose that’s closer to what my wife has been worried about. I know how it’s going to sound when I say it out loud, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic, but there are these old ballads my brother kept—songs he cared about—and I’ve been going over them because of the memorial. And the more I listen, or read the words, the more I’m sure there are warnings in them.\n\nNot just memories, not just “oh, this reminds me of him.” I mean warnings meant for me. Certain lines stand out in a way they didn’t before, like he left something there, or like I’m supposed to understand what to do from them. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, that they’re just songs and grief makes people connect things. I understand why she’d say that. But I know what I heard in those words. It isn’t random to me.\n\nAnd I know you might say that sounds unrealistic, but it feels very clear when I’m with them. Like there’s a message tucked underneath the plain meaning, and if I ignore it, I’d be missing something important from my brother.\n\nDoctor: Did all of this start after your brother died?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it really did. I mean, not the very minute he died, if you know what I mean, but it all began after that. It’s been about two months now, since he passed, and especially since I started working on the tribute for him.\n\nAt first it just felt like normal grieving, trying to pull things together and make the memorial mean something. I was going through things connected to him, thinking about what would honor him properly, and then it sort of… grew from there. The songs, the meaning of them, the feeling that I needed to get it right—that all started in that period after his death.\n\nSo yes, I’d say it’s been going on for around two months, tied pretty directly to losing him and planning the memorial.\n\nDoctor: Have you been talking more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t always notice it while it’s happening, but people have pointed it out.\n\nLike at the senior center meetings, if we start talking about the memorial, I’ll begin explaining the songs, and then that leads into the quilts, and then the photos, and how each one fits with my brother, and before I know it I’ve gone on and on. I’m not trying to take over, but I’ll realize I’ve talked right over two or three people who were trying to add something.\n\nMy pastor has had to sort of gently step in and say, “Aaron, let’s let someone else speak,” which is embarrassing when I think about it afterward. At the time, though, it feels like I need to get the whole thought out before it disappears or before someone misunderstands what I mean. Yes, I have. That’s actually something my wife keeps bringing up too, because I used to be able to sit in my chair for a while, you know, have my coffee, read the paper, maybe doze off a little in the afternoon. It’s more… it just keeps taking up space because there’s always another detail, another idea. I wouldn’t want to say that exactly if I don’t know. It’s hard for me to separate.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more important or specially capable than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. “More important” sounds a little… big, like I’m walking around thinking I’m better than people, and I don’t think that’s what I feel. My wife might say I’m acting like I’m the only one who can handle it, but from inside it just feels like I’m trying to do it properly.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed feeling powerful or superior or anything like that. I don’t know… I guess I’m not sure what counts as risky in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t, like, gone out and done something wild on purpose, if that’s what you’re asking. I’m not thinking of anything where I’d say, “That was completely unlike me and dangerous.” Mostly I’ve just been wrapped up in getting things right, and maybe I’ve pushed harder than people around me would like. But when I’m doing it, it doesn’t feel risky exactly—it feels necessary, or like I’m trying to finish what I started.\n\nOut of character… maybe my wife would have a better answer, honestly. She might say I’ve been more intense or stubborn than usual. But from my side, I can’t point to some big thing, like spending a bunch of money or taking off somewhere or doing something reckless. I don’t think so. Nothing like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you started to believe things about the memorial or your brother that other people say aren’t true or seem unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose that’s closer to what my wife has been worried about. Certain lines stand out in a way they didn’t before, like he left something there, or like I’m supposed to understand what to do from them. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, it has. I don’t like saying that, because in my mind I keep telling myself this is for my brother, so it’s important, and people should understand that. But if I’m honest, it’s gotten in the way of ordinary life.

I’ve missed my walking group several times, and I used to be pretty regular with that. Same with book club — I just don’t go, or I tell myself I’ll go after I finish one more thing for the memorial display, and then the time passes. The garden’s been neglected too. My wife pointed that out to me because I’ve always kept up with it, even if it was just a little trimming or watering, and now it’s just sitting there because all my attention goes to these photos and notes and arrangements.

And at the senior center… I think I’ve upset some people. I don’t mean to, but every conversation somehow turns into the memorial. Someone will mention something completely different, and I’ll bring it back to my brother or the display or what song should be played, and I can see people get tired or uncomfortable. A couple friends have pulled back, I think.

My wife says our whole day has become about either calming me down or trying to redirect me — stopping me from making another phone call, or taking another trip over to the center, or getting into another argument about how things should be set up. She says it’s like there’s no normal day anymore, just managing whatever I’m focused on next. That hurt to hear, but… I suppose she’s not wrong.

It’s more… it just keeps taking up space because there’s always another detail, another idea. Sometimes I don’t even realize I’ve gone back to it until I’ve spent an hour making notes or looking something up. So, I don’t know. It keeps coming up, yes, but I’m not sure it feels like an intrusive thing in the way you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually cheerful or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… cheerful maybe isn’t exactly the word I’d use, but there are times lately where I feel, I don’t know, lit up. Like everything about this memorial suddenly feels so clear and important, like I’ve got this purpose and if I can just get it right, then people in town are finally going to understand my brother, and understand our family, and what all of it meant.\n\nAnd when I’m in that feeling, it can feel almost good—more than good, really, like I’m carrying something that matters. But it doesn’t stay soft or pleasant. If my wife asks me to slow down, or says maybe the display doesn’t need another section, I can snap at her. Same with people at the senior center, if they question how I’m setting things up or they don’t move fast enough. I get irritated quick, like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\nSo… yes, there’s been this high feeling, but it’s mixed with me getting sharp with people too.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still feeling full of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, my sleep has been a bit all over the place, but I haven’t been keeping track of it the way maybe I should. Some nights I’m up later than I meant to be because I get caught up thinking through details or writing something down, and then I look at the clock and it’s later than I expected.\n\nBut whether I’m needing much less sleep and still feeling full of energy… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t want to say that exactly if I don’t know. There are times I keep going when my wife thinks I should stop, and I’ll tell her I’m fine, I’ve got things to finish, but I also don’t know how much of that is energy and how much is just me not wanting to leave things undone.\n\nSo… maybe I’ve been staying up more, but I haven’t really noticed it as “I don’t need sleep anymore” or anything like that. It’s hard for me to separate.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more important or specially capable than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. “More important” sounds a little… big, like I’m walking around thinking I’m better than people, and I don’t think that’s what I feel. At least I don’t notice myself thinking that.\n\nI do feel like what I’m doing matters, maybe more than other people seem to understand, and sometimes that makes me feel like I’m the one who has to make sure it comes out right. But specially capable? I don’t know. I’ve always been particular about things when they matter to me. My wife might say I’m acting like I’m the only one who can handle it, but from inside it just feels like I’m trying to do it properly.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed feeling powerful or superior or anything like that. More like I feel responsible, and maybe I’m holding on to it too tightly.\n\nDoctor: Have you done anything lately that felt out of character or risky for you?\n\nPatient: Risky? I don’t know… I guess I’m not sure what counts as risky in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t, like, gone out and done something wild on purpose, if that’s what you’re asking. I’m not thinking of anything where I’d say, “That was completely unlike me and dangerous.” Mostly I’ve just been wrapped up in getting things right, and maybe I’ve pushed harder than people around me would like. But when I’m doing it, it doesn’t feel risky exactly—it feels necessary, or like I’m trying to finish what I started.\n\nOut of character… maybe my wife would have a better answer, honestly. She might say I’ve been more intense or stubborn than usual. But from my side, I can’t point to some big thing, like spending a bunch of money or taking off somewhere or doing something reckless. I don’t think so. Nothing like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you started to believe things about the memorial or your brother that other people say aren’t true or seem unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose that’s closer to what my wife has been worried about. I know how it’s going to sound when I say it out loud, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic, but there are these old ballads my brother kept—songs he cared about—and I’ve been going over them because of the memorial. And the more I listen, or read the words, the more I’m sure there are warnings in them.\n\nNot just memories, not just “oh, this reminds me of him.” I mean warnings meant for me. Certain lines stand out in a way they didn’t before, like he left something there, or like I’m supposed to understand what to do from them. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, that they’re just songs and grief makes people connect things. I understand why she’d say that. But I know what I heard in those words. It isn’t random to me.\n\nAnd I know you might say that sounds unrealistic, but it feels very clear when I’m with them. Like there’s a message tucked underneath the plain meaning, and if I ignore it, I’d be missing something important from my brother.\n\nDoctor: Did all of this start after your brother died?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it really did. I mean, not the very minute he died, if you know what I mean, but it all began after that. It’s been about two months now, since he passed, and especially since I started working on the tribute for him.\n\nAt first it just felt like normal grieving, trying to pull things together and make the memorial mean something. I was going through things connected to him, thinking about what would honor him properly, and then it sort of… grew from there. The songs, the meaning of them, the feeling that I needed to get it right—that all started in that period after his death.\n\nSo yes, I’d say it’s been going on for around two months, tied pretty directly to losing him and planning the memorial.\n\nDoctor: Have you been talking more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t always notice it while it’s happening, but people have pointed it out.\n\nLike at the senior center meetings, if we start talking about the memorial, I’ll begin explaining the songs, and then that leads into the quilts, and then the photos, and how each one fits with my brother, and before I know it I’ve gone on and on. I’m not trying to take over, but I’ll realize I’ve talked right over two or three people who were trying to add something.\n\nMy pastor has had to sort of gently step in and say, “Aaron, let’s let someone else speak,” which is embarrassing when I think about it afterward. At the time, though, it feels like I need to get the whole thought out before it disappears or before someone misunderstands what I mean. So yes, I suppose I have been talking more than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more active than usual lately, always starting projects or feeling driven to keep doing things?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. That’s actually something my wife keeps bringing up too, because I used to be able to sit in my chair for a while, you know, have my coffee, read the paper, maybe doze off a little in the afternoon. Lately I can’t seem to do that. I’ll sit down and then two minutes later I’m back up again because I remembered a box that needs sorting or a phone call I ought to make, or I’ll think the papers for the memorial aren’t in the right order and I need to rearrange them.\n\nI’ve been hauling boxes around the house, moving things from one room to another, spreading photographs and notes out on the table, then deciding they belong somewhere else. I’ll walk from the living room to the kitchen and back again, then down the hall, then back to the table, like I’m following some thread I can’t quite hold onto unless I keep moving.\n\nIt feels like my body has more steam in it than a man my age ought to have. I’m sixty-nine, I know I shouldn’t be running myself like this, but when I try to stop, it feels wrong, like there’s still something unfinished pressing on me. So yes, more active than usual, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Have these changes caused serious problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it has. I don’t like saying that, because in my mind I keep telling myself this is for my brother, so it’s important, and people should understand that. But if I’m honest, it’s gotten in the way of ordinary life.\n\nI’ve missed my walking group several times, and I used to be pretty regular with that. Same with book club — I just don’t go, or I tell myself I’ll go after I finish one more thing for the memorial display, and then the time passes. The garden’s been neglected too. My wife pointed that out to me because I’ve always kept up with it, even if it was just a little trimming or watering, and now it’s just sitting there because all my attention goes to these photos and notes and arrangements.\n\nAnd at the senior center… I think I’ve upset some people. I don’t mean to, but every conversation somehow turns into the memorial. Someone will mention something completely different, and I’ll bring it back to my brother or the display or what song should be played, and I can see people get tired or uncomfortable. A couple friends have pulled back, I think.\n\nMy wife says our whole day has become about either calming me down or trying to redirect me — stopping me from making another phone call, or taking another trip over to the center, or getting into another argument about how things should be set up. She says it’s like there’s no normal day anymore, just managing whatever I’m focused on next. That hurt to hear, but… I suppose she’s not wrong.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

Adjustment disorder/grief reactions are less likely given syndromal manic features with possible psychotic symptoms.","is_final":false}

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I have. I almost don’t like saying it out loud because it sounds strange, but yes.

It happens mostly when I’m at the kitchen table with his song sheets spread out in front of me. I’ll be sorting through them, trying to put them in the right order, and I’ll hear him — my brother — clear as day. Sometimes it’s like he’s behind me humming one of the tunes, just soft, like he used to do when he was thinking. Other times it’s more like he’s telling me which song comes next, like, “No, Aaron, that one goes after this one,” and I’ll turn or pause because it feels so real.

But nobody else hears it. My wife will be in the house and I’ll ask, “Did you hear that?” or I’ll look up like someone just spoke, and she’ll say she didn’t hear anything. And I know he’s gone, I know that, but in the moment it doesn’t feel like a memory exactly. It feels like him, right there, helping me with the music.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess the main thing is this memorial I’ve been trying to put together for my brother. I keep finding more things that need doing—songs he liked, people I should call, displays I could make, places tied to him I ought to visit or include somehow—and before I know it, whole days are gone.\n\nMy wife says I’ve been spending most of my time chasing all of that down, calling folks, planning little trips, arranging things in my head and on paper, and she says I’ve pushed aside everything else. The garden’s been sitting there, I haven’t been going with my walking group like I usually do, and honestly a lot of the regular day-to-day stuff has just kind of fallen away. So… that’s what brings me in, I suppose. It’s gotten hard to slow it down.\n\nDoctor: Do unwanted thoughts about the memorial keep popping into your mind even when you try not to think about it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call them “unwanted thoughts,” exactly. It’s more like once I start thinking about the memorial, one thing leads to another and then I’m off in it again. Like I’ll see something in the house, or remember a song, or think of someone who knew my brother, and then I start wondering whether that should be part of it somehow.\n\nI can’t say I’ve really sat there trying not to think about it and had it force its way in, if that’s what you mean. It’s more… it just keeps taking up space because there’s always another detail, another idea. Sometimes I don’t even realize I’ve gone back to it until I’ve spent an hour making notes or looking something up. So, I don’t know. It keeps coming up, yes, but I’m not sure it feels like an intrusive thing in the way you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually cheerful or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… cheerful maybe isn’t exactly the word I’d use, but there are times lately where I feel, I don’t know, lit up. Like everything about this memorial suddenly feels so clear and important, like I’ve got this purpose and if I can just get it right, then people in town are finally going to understand my brother, and understand our family, and what all of it meant.\n\nAnd when I’m in that feeling, it can feel almost good—more than good, really, like I’m carrying something that matters. But it doesn’t stay soft or pleasant. If my wife asks me to slow down, or says maybe the display doesn’t need another section, I can snap at her. Same with people at the senior center, if they question how I’m setting things up or they don’t move fast enough. I get irritated quick, like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\nSo… yes, there’s been this high feeling, but it’s mixed with me getting sharp with people too.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still feeling full of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, my sleep has been a bit all over the place, but I haven’t been keeping track of it the way maybe I should. Some nights I’m up later than I meant to be because I get caught up thinking through details or writing something down, and then I look at the clock and it’s later than I expected.\n\nBut whether I’m needing much less sleep and still feeling full of energy… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t want to say that exactly if I don’t know. There are times I keep going when my wife thinks I should stop, and I’ll tell her I’m fine, I’ve got things to finish, but I also don’t know how much of that is energy and how much is just me not wanting to leave things undone.\n\nSo… maybe I’ve been staying up more, but I haven’t really noticed it as “I don’t need sleep anymore” or anything like that. It’s hard for me to separate.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more important or specially capable than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. “More important” sounds a little… big, like I’m walking around thinking I’m better than people, and I don’t think that’s what I feel. At least I don’t notice myself thinking that.\n\nI do feel like what I’m doing matters, maybe more than other people seem to understand, and sometimes that makes me feel like I’m the one who has to make sure it comes out right. But specially capable? I don’t know. I’ve always been particular about things when they matter to me. My wife might say I’m acting like I’m the only one who can handle it, but from inside it just feels like I’m trying to do it properly.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed feeling powerful or superior or anything like that. More like I feel responsible, and maybe I’m holding on to it too tightly.\n\nDoctor: Have you done anything lately that felt out of character or risky for you?\n\nPatient: Risky? I don’t know… I guess I’m not sure what counts as risky in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t, like, gone out and done something wild on purpose, if that’s what you’re asking. I’m not thinking of anything where I’d say, “That was completely unlike me and dangerous.” Mostly I’ve just been wrapped up in getting things right, and maybe I’ve pushed harder than people around me would like. But when I’m doing it, it doesn’t feel risky exactly—it feels necessary, or like I’m trying to finish what I started.\n\nOut of character… maybe my wife would have a better answer, honestly. She might say I’ve been more intense or stubborn than usual. But from my side, I can’t point to some big thing, like spending a bunch of money or taking off somewhere or doing something reckless. I don’t think so. Nothing like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you started to believe things about the memorial or your brother that other people say aren’t true or seem unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose that’s closer to what my wife has been worried about. I know how it’s going to sound when I say it out loud, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic, but there are these old ballads my brother kept—songs he cared about—and I’ve been going over them because of the memorial. And the more I listen, or read the words, the more I’m sure there are warnings in them.\n\nNot just memories, not just “oh, this reminds me of him.” I mean warnings meant for me. Certain lines stand out in a way they didn’t before, like he left something there, or like I’m supposed to understand what to do from them. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, that they’re just songs and grief makes people connect things. I understand why she’d say that. But I know what I heard in those words. It isn’t random to me.\n\nAnd I know you might say that sounds unrealistic, but it feels very clear when I’m with them. Like there’s a message tucked underneath the plain meaning, and if I ignore it, I’d be missing something important from my brother.\n\nDoctor: Did all of this start after your brother died?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it really did. I mean, not the very minute he died, if you know what I mean, but it all began after that. It’s been about two months now, since he passed, and especially since I started working on the tribute for him.\n\nAt first it just felt like normal grieving, trying to pull things together and make the memorial mean something. I was going through things connected to him, thinking about what would honor him properly, and then it sort of… grew from there. The songs, the meaning of them, the feeling that I needed to get it right—that all started in that period after his death.\n\nSo yes, I’d say it’s been going on for around two months, tied pretty directly to losing him and planning the memorial.\n\nDoctor: Have you been talking more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t always notice it while it’s happening, but people have pointed it out.\n\nLike at the senior center meetings, if we start talking about the memorial, I’ll begin explaining the songs, and then that leads into the quilts, and then the photos, and how each one fits with my brother, and before I know it I’ve gone on and on. I’m not trying to take over, but I’ll realize I’ve talked right over two or three people who were trying to add something.\n\nMy pastor has had to sort of gently step in and say, “Aaron, let’s let someone else speak,” which is embarrassing when I think about it afterward. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, that they’re just songs and grief makes people connect things. I understand why she’d say that. But I know what I heard in those words. It isn’t random to me.\n\nAnd I know you might say that sounds unrealistic, but it feels very clear when I’m with them. Like there’s a message tucked underneath the plain meaning, and if I ignore it, I’d be missing something important from my brother.\n\nDoctor: Did all of this start after your brother died?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it really did. I mean, not the very minute he died, if you know what I mean, but it all began after that. It’s been about two months now, since he passed, and especially since I started working on the tribute for him.\n\nAt first it just felt like normal grieving, trying to pull things together and make the memorial mean something. I was going through things connected to him, thinking about what would honor him properly, and then it sort of… grew from there. The songs, the meaning of them, the feeling that I needed to get it right—that all started in that period after his death.\n\nSo yes, I’d say it’s been going on for around two months, tied pretty directly to losing him and planning the memorial.\n\nDoctor: Have you been talking more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t always notice it while it’s happening, but people have pointed it out.\n\nLike at the senior center meetings, if we start talking about the memorial, I’ll begin explaining the songs, and then that leads into the quilts, and then the photos, and how each one fits with my brother, and before I know it I’ve gone on and on. I’m not trying to take over, but I’ll realize I’ve talked right over two or three people who were trying to add something.\n\nMy pastor has had to sort of gently step in and say, “Aaron, let’s let someone else speak,” which is embarrassing when I think about it afterward. At the time, though, it feels like I need to get the whole thought out before it disappears or before someone misunderstands what I mean. So yes, I suppose I have been talking more than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more active than usual lately, always starting projects or feeling driven to keep doing things?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. That’s actually something my wife keeps bringing up too, because I used to be able to sit in my chair for a while, you know, have my coffee, read the paper, maybe doze off a little in the afternoon. Lately I can’t seem to do that. I’ll sit down and then two minutes later I’m back up again because I remembered a box that needs sorting or a phone call I ought to make, or I’ll think the papers for the memorial aren’t in the right order and I need to rearrange them.\n\nI’ve been hauling boxes around the house, moving things from one room to another, spreading photographs and notes out on the table, then deciding they belong somewhere else. I’ll walk from the living room to the kitchen and back again, then down the hall, then back to the table, like I’m following some thread I can’t quite hold onto unless I keep moving.\n\nIt feels like my body has more steam in it than a man my age ought to have. I’m sixty-nine, I know I shouldn’t be running myself like this, but when I try to stop, it feels wrong, like there’s still something unfinished pressing on me. So yes, more active than usual, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Have these changes caused serious problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it has. I don’t like saying that, because in my mind I keep telling myself this is for my brother, so it’s important, and people should understand that. But if I’m honest, it’s gotten in the way of ordinary life.\n\nI’ve missed my walking group several times, and I used to be pretty regular with that. Same with book club — I just don’t go, or I tell myself I’ll go after I finish one more thing for the memorial display, and then the time passes. The garden’s been neglected too. My wife pointed that out to me because I’ve always kept up with it, even if it was just a little trimming or watering, and now it’s just sitting there because all my attention goes to these photos and notes and arrangements.\n\nAnd at the senior center… I think I’ve upset some people. I don’t mean to, but every conversation somehow turns into the memorial. Someone will mention something completely different, and I’ll bring it back to my brother or the display or what song should be played, and I can see people get tired or uncomfortable. A couple friends have pulled back, I think.\n\nMy wife says our whole day has become about either calming me down or trying to redirect me — stopping me from making another phone call, or taking another trip over to the center, or getting into another argument about how things should be set up. She says it’s like there’s no normal day anymore, just managing whatever I’m focused on next. That hurt to hear, but… I suppose she’s not wrong.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard your brother speaking to you when no one else was there?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I almost don’t like saying it out loud because it sounds strange, but yes.\n\nIt happens mostly when I’m at the kitchen table with his song sheets spread out in front of me. I’ll be sorting through them, trying to put them in the right order, and I’ll hear him — my brother — clear as day. Sometimes it’s like he’s behind me humming one of the tunes, just soft, like he used to do when he was thinking. Other times it’s more like he’s telling me which song comes next, like, “No, Aaron, that one goes after this one,” and I’ll turn or pause because it feels so real.\n\nBut nobody else hears it. My wife will be in the house and I’ll ask, “Did you hear that?” or I’ll look up like someone just spoke, and she’ll say she didn’t hear anything. And I know he’s gone, I know that, but in the moment it doesn’t feel like a memory exactly. It feels like him, right there, helping me with the music.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. No… not like this. I mean, I’ve had busy times in my life, of course. Raising a family, working, getting through different troubles, you have periods where you’re running around and there’s a lot to do. But before my brother died, I don’t remember ever having this kind of… spell, I guess, where I felt pushed along like this, like I couldn’t leave things alone.

And the voice part — no. I never heard him in the house before. I never sat at the kitchen table and heard him humming behind me or telling me what order the songs should go in. That all started after he passed, when I got so wrapped up in the memorial work.

Same with the songs feeling like they were aimed at me somehow, like they were warning me or telling me something personal. Before all this, songs were just songs. They might make me sad or remind me of something, sure, but they weren’t sending me messages. That feeling came along with this whole burst of energy and the planning and rearranging and not being able to stop. So no, I can’t say there was another time like this before.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF31.2, F30.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess the main thing is this memorial I’ve been trying to put together for my brother. It started off as something simple, you know, just a way to honor him, maybe have some music and a few pictures, but it’s gotten much bigger than I meant for it to. I keep finding more things that need doing—songs he liked, people I should call, displays I could make, places tied to him I ought to visit or include somehow—and before I know it, whole days are gone.\n\nMy wife says I’ve been spending most of my time chasing all of that down, calling folks, planning little trips, arranging things in my head and on paper, and she says I’ve pushed aside everything else. The garden’s been sitting there, I haven’t been going with my walking group like I usually do, and honestly a lot of the regular day-to-day stuff has just kind of fallen away. So… that’s what brings me in, I suppose. It’s gotten hard to slow it down.\n\nDoctor: Do unwanted thoughts about the memorial keep popping into your mind even when you try not to think about it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call them “unwanted thoughts,” exactly. It’s more like once I start thinking about the memorial, one thing leads to another and then I’m off in it again. Like I’ll see something in the house, or remember a song, or think of someone who knew my brother, and then I start wondering whether that should be part of it somehow.\n\nI can’t say I’ve really sat there trying not to think about it and had it force its way in, if that’s what you mean. It’s more… it just keeps taking up space because there’s always another detail, another idea. Sometimes I don’t even realize I’ve gone back to it until I’ve spent an hour making notes or looking something up. So, I don’t know. It keeps coming up, yes, but I’m not sure it feels like an intrusive thing in the way you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually cheerful or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… cheerful maybe isn’t exactly the word I’d use, but there are times lately where I feel, I don’t know, lit up. Like everything about this memorial suddenly feels so clear and important, like I’ve got this purpose and if I can just get it right, then people in town are finally going to understand my brother, and understand our family, and what all of it meant.\n\nAnd when I’m in that feeling, it can feel almost good—more than good, really, like I’m carrying something that matters. But it doesn’t stay soft or pleasant. If my wife asks me to slow down, or says maybe the display doesn’t need another section, I can snap at her. Same with people at the senior center, if they question how I’m setting things up or they don’t move fast enough. I get irritated quick, like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\nSo… yes, there’s been this high feeling, but it’s mixed with me getting sharp with people too.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been needing much less sleep than usual and still feeling full of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, my sleep has been a bit all over the place, but I haven’t been keeping track of it the way maybe I should. Some nights I’m up later than I meant to be because I get caught up thinking through details or writing something down, and then I look at the clock and it’s later than I expected.\n\nBut whether I’m needing much less sleep and still feeling full of energy… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t want to say that exactly if I don’t know. There are times I keep going when my wife thinks I should stop, and I’ll tell her I’m fine, I’ve got things to finish, but I also don’t know how much of that is energy and how much is just me not wanting to leave things undone.\n\nSo… maybe I’ve been staying up more, but I haven’t really noticed it as “I don’t need sleep anymore” or anything like that. It’s hard for me to separate.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more important or specially capable than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. “More important” sounds a little… big, like I’m walking around thinking I’m better than people, and I don’t think that’s what I feel. At least I don’t notice myself thinking that.\n\nI do feel like what I’m doing matters, maybe more than other people seem to understand, and sometimes that makes me feel like I’m the one who has to make sure it comes out right. But specially capable? I don’t know. I’ve always been particular about things when they matter to me. My wife might say I’m acting like I’m the only one who can handle it, but from inside it just feels like I’m trying to do it properly.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed feeling powerful or superior or anything like that. More like I feel responsible, and maybe I’m holding on to it too tightly.\n\nDoctor: Have you done anything lately that felt out of character or risky for you?\n\nPatient: Risky? I don’t know… I guess I’m not sure what counts as risky in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t, like, gone out and done something wild on purpose, if that’s what you’re asking. I’m not thinking of anything where I’d say, “That was completely unlike me and dangerous.” Mostly I’ve just been wrapped up in getting things right, and maybe I’ve pushed harder than people around me would like. But when I’m doing it, it doesn’t feel risky exactly—it feels necessary, or like I’m trying to finish what I started.\n\nOut of character… maybe my wife would have a better answer, honestly. She might say I’ve been more intense or stubborn than usual. But from my side, I can’t point to some big thing, like spending a bunch of money or taking off somewhere or doing something reckless. I don’t think so. Nothing like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you started to believe things about the memorial or your brother that other people say aren’t true or seem unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose that’s closer to what my wife has been worried about. I know how it’s going to sound when I say it out loud, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic, but there are these old ballads my brother kept—songs he cared about—and I’ve been going over them because of the memorial. And the more I listen, or read the words, the more I’m sure there are warnings in them.\n\nNot just memories, not just “oh, this reminds me of him.” I mean warnings meant for me. Certain lines stand out in a way they didn’t before, like he left something there, or like I’m supposed to understand what to do from them. My wife says I’m reading too much into it, that they’re just songs and grief makes people connect things. I understand why she’d say that. But I know what I heard in those words. It isn’t random to me.\n\nAnd I know you might say that sounds unrealistic, but it feels very clear when I’m with them. Like there’s a message tucked underneath the plain meaning, and if I ignore it, I’d be missing something important from my brother.\n\nDoctor: Did all of this start after your brother died?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it really did. I mean, not the very minute he died, if you know what I mean, but it all began after that. It’s been about two months now, since he passed, and especially since I started working on the tribute for him.\n\nAt first it just felt like normal grieving, trying to pull things together and make the memorial mean something. I was going through things connected to him, thinking about what would honor him properly, and then it sort of… grew from there. The songs, the meaning of them, the feeling that I needed to get it right—that all started in that period after his death.\n\nSo yes, I’d say it’s been going on for around two months, tied pretty directly to losing him and planning the memorial.\n\nDoctor: Have you been talking more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t always notice it while it’s happening, but people have pointed it out.\n\nLike at the senior center meetings, if we start talking about the memorial, I’ll begin explaining the songs, and then that leads into the quilts, and then the photos, and how each one fits with my brother, and before I know it I’ve gone on and on. I’m not trying to take over, but I’ll realize I’ve talked right over two or three people who were trying to add something.\n\nMy pastor has had to sort of gently step in and say, “Aaron, let’s let someone else speak,” which is embarrassing when I think about it afterward. At the time, though, it feels like I need to get the whole thought out before it disappears or before someone misunderstands what I mean. So yes, I suppose I have been talking more than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more active than usual lately, always starting projects or feeling driven to keep doing things?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. That’s actually something my wife keeps bringing up too, because I used to be able to sit in my chair for a while, you know, have my coffee, read the paper, maybe doze off a little in the afternoon. Lately I can’t seem to do that. I’ll sit down and then two minutes later I’m back up again because I remembered a box that needs sorting or a phone call I ought to make, or I’ll think the papers for the memorial aren’t in the right order and I need to rearrange them.\n\nI’ve been hauling boxes around the house, moving things from one room to another, spreading photographs and notes out on the table, then deciding they belong somewhere else. I’ll walk from the living room to the kitchen and back again, then down the hall, then back to the table, like I’m following some thread I can’t quite hold onto unless I keep moving.\n\nIt feels like my body has more steam in it than a man my age ought to have. I’m sixty-nine, I know I shouldn’t be running myself like this, but when I try to stop, it feels wrong, like there’s still something unfinished pressing on me. So yes, more active than usual, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Have these changes caused serious problems in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it has. I don’t like saying that, because in my mind I keep telling myself this is for my brother, so it’s important, and people should understand that. But if I’m honest, it’s gotten in the way of ordinary life.\n\nI’ve missed my walking group several times, and I used to be pretty regular with that. Same with book club — I just don’t go, or I tell myself I’ll go after I finish one more thing for the memorial display, and then the time passes. The garden’s been neglected too. My wife pointed that out to me because I’ve always kept up with it, even if it was just a little trimming or watering, and now it’s just sitting there because all my attention goes to these photos and notes and arrangements.\n\nAnd at the senior center… I think I’ve upset some people. I don’t mean to, but every conversation somehow turns into the memorial. Someone will mention something completely different, and I’ll bring it back to my brother or the display or what song should be played, and I can see people get tired or uncomfortable. A couple friends have pulled back, I think.\n\nMy wife says our whole day has become about either calming me down or trying to redirect me — stopping me from making another phone call, or taking another trip over to the center, or getting into another argument about how things should be set up. She says it’s like there’s no normal day anymore, just managing whatever I’m focused on next. That hurt to hear, but… I suppose she’s not wrong.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard your brother speaking to you when no one else was there?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I almost don’t like saying it out loud because it sounds strange, but yes.\n\nIt happens mostly when I’m at the kitchen table with his song sheets spread out in front of me. I’ll be sorting through them, trying to put them in the right order, and I’ll hear him — my brother — clear as day. Sometimes it’s like he’s behind me humming one of the tunes, just soft, like he used to do when he was thinking. Other times it’s more like he’s telling me which song comes next, like, “No, Aaron, that one goes after this one,” and I’ll turn or pause because it feels so real.\n\nBut nobody else hears it. My wife will be in the house and I’ll ask, “Did you hear that?” or I’ll look up like someone just spoke, and she’ll say she didn’t hear anything. And I know he’s gone, I know that, but in the moment it doesn’t feel like a memory exactly. It feels like him, right there, helping me with the music.\n\nDoctor: Before your brother died, was there ever another time in your life when you had a similar spell of feeling unusually energized or driven, talking more than usual, or having experiences like this?\n\nPatient: No… not like this. I mean, I’ve had busy times in my life, of course. Raising a family, working, getting through different troubles, you have periods where you’re running around and there’s a lot to do. But before my brother died, I don’t remember ever having this kind of… spell, I guess, where I felt pushed along like this, like I couldn’t leave things alone.\n\nAnd the voice part — no. I never heard him in the house before. I never sat at the kitchen table and heard him humming behind me or telling me what order the songs should go in. That all started after he passed, when I got so wrapped up in the memorial work.\n\nSame with the songs feeling like they were aimed at me somehow, like they were warning me or telling me something personal. Before all this, songs were just songs. They might make me sad or remind me of something, sure, but they weren’t sending me messages. That feeling came along with this whole burst of energy and the planning and rearranging and not being able to stop. So no, I can’t say there was another time like this before.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

Before this? No, not really in the same way. I mean, I’ve had days where I was in a good mood or busy with something, but not this feeling of being… lit up, I guess. Full of purpose. Like I suddenly know exactly what has to be done and everybody else just needs to understand it.

But then if my wife says, “Maybe leave that alone for tonight,” or someone at the senior center questions where I put something, I can snap. I don’t like admitting that. I hear my voice get sharp, and I’ll think, why are they slowing me down, don’t they see this matters?

So before now, I don’t remember having that sort of high, driven feeling mixed with getting irritated so quickly. This has been different.
